Path B · No CloudVision

  1. Write the Studios inputs above as a one-page requirements sheet (fabric, services, host ports, external). This is the hand-off document a Studios engineer would work from, and it is exactly what the AVD files in 7.3 encode.

  2. Do 7.3 and come back: for each row of the mapping table, find the line in labs/avd/intended/configs/leaf3.cfg it produced. When you can do that from memory, you will read any studio form correctly.

  3. Simulate a workspace: make the VLAN 40 change on a git branch, build, review the diff (your "Review Workspace"), and only then deploy (your "Submit" plus change control). 7.3 task 8 walks through it.

Exam tip. Studios work is mostly typing values into the right field and getting the tag queries right. The two classic time sinks are (1) a tag query that matches nothing or the wrong devices (always open the right arrow and look at the matched list), and (2) a workspace that builds with warnings you ignored, so the change control pushes less than you think. Read the build result and the proposed config for one device of each role before you submit. Also know that a workspace can be abandoned: if you get lost, start a clean workspace instead of untangling a broken one.
Real world. Studios suit teams who want guard-rails and a UI; AVD suits teams who want git, code review and CI. Many shops run both: AVD for the data center fabric, Studios for campus. The concepts (pools, roles, tags, services, endpoints) are the same, so the skill transfers.

Break / fix

Inject: in EVPN Services, change the VTEP query for VLAN 20 to DC:L5DC AND Leaf-Domain:POD_A, then build, submit and execute.

Symptom: the change control succeeds, but host2 can no longer reach 10.20.20.1 or anything in VLAN 20 from VLAN 10. host1 is fine.

Root cause and fix

The query now matches only leaf1 and leaf2, so the studio removed VLAN 20, Vlan20 and the VNI 10020 mapping from leaf3 and leaf4. Nothing failed technically; the intent was wrong. The proposed-changes screen showed no vlan 20 and no interface Vlan20 for leaf3/leaf4, which is why you always read it. Fix the query back to DC:L5DC AND Role:Leaf in a new workspace and submit. On the CLI, show vxlan vni on leaf3 shows VNI 10020 missing.

7.3 AVD and ANTA: the same fabric as code

Arista Validated Designs (AVD) turns a short YAML description of the fabric into complete EOS configurations, documentation and a test catalog. ANTA (Arista Network Test Automation) runs those tests against the live switches. Neither is named on the exam datasheet, but the L3LS-EVPN Studio is built on the AVD data model, every config in this lab guide came from AVD, and this is how EVPN fabrics are built and checked in practice. Everything here runs on the free containerlab fabric.

The repo uses pyavd (AVD's Python library) through labs/avd/build.py, so you need no Ansible. The Ansible way is shown too, because most production teams and the AVD docs use it.

  1. Read the inputs. For each file below, say in one sentence what it controls, then predict three lines of leaf3.cfg it produces.

    FileWhat it holdsLines it produces (examples)
    inventory.ymlThe hosts and the group tree: L5DC > L5DC_FABRIC > L5DC_SPINES / L5DC_LEAVES; L5DC_LEAVES is also a child of L5DC_NETWORK_SERVICES and L5DC_CONNECTED_ENDPOINTS, which is how the leaves inherit services and host ports. Group membership decides which group_vars file applies to which device.None directly; it decides who gets what
    group_vars/L5DC.ymlSettings for every switch: Ansible connection variables (eAPI over HTTPS), fabric_name, local user, management interface and gateway, DNS, NTP and the P2P uplink MTU.username admin ..., ip route 0.0.0.0/0 172.100.100.1, ntp server 0.pool.ntp.org prefer, ip name-server vrf default 1.1.1.1
    group_vars/L5DC_FABRIC.ymlThe topology: underlay and overlay protocol (eBGP/eBGP), the spine and l3leaf node types with their pools, ASNs, node ids, uplink ports, MLAG ports, virtual router MAC and STP settings, and the two node groups POD_A and POD_B (which makes them MLAG pairs). It also carries a structured_config block (YAML anchor &mgmt_apis, reused on spine and l3leaf defaults) that adds eAPI and gNMI, so a full config replace never removes your management APIs.router bgp 65102, management api http-commands, ip address 10.255.255.9/31, mlag configuration / domain-id POD_B, ip address 10.255.1.5/32 on Loopback1
    group_vars/L5DC_SPINES.ymlJust type: spine: tells eos_designs which node type the group is.spanning-tree mode none, the next-hop-unchanged on the spine EVPN peer group
    group_vars/L5DC_LEAVES.ymlJust type: l3leaf.VTEP, MLAG and services only render on this type
    group_vars/L5DC_NETWORK_SERVICES.ymlTenants: TENANT_A with VRF TENANT-A (VNI 50001, MLAG iBGP VLAN 3001, diagnostic Loopback100), SVIs 10 and 20 with anycast IPs, L2 VLAN 30, and the border L3 interfaces and BGP peers to core1 on leaf3/leaf4.vxlan vlan 20 vni 10020, ip address virtual 10.20.20.1/24, neighbor 10.255.254.1 remote-as 65000
    group_vars/L5DC_CONNECTED_ENDPOINTS.ymlServers: host1 on leaf1/leaf2 Ethernet5 and host2 on leaf3/leaf4 Ethernet5, LACP trunk with VLANs 10,20,30.interface Port-Channel5 / mlag 5, channel-group 5 mode active

  2. Build the configs with pyavd and confirm what was written: structured configs (YAML), EOS configs, and one ANTA catalog per device.

  3. Compare intended/structured_configs/leaf3.yml with intended/configs/leaf3.cfg. Explain the two-stage design (eos_designs produces structured config; eos_cli_config_gen renders CLI) and why it matters for troubleshooting a wrong line.

  4. Do the same build with Ansible: install the collection, write a playbook with arista.avd.eos_designs and arista.avd.eos_cli_config_gen, run it, and confirm the configs are identical to the pyavd build.

  5. Deploy the generated configs to the containerlab fabric so that each switch ends up exactly as designed (full replace, not merge). Before you do, confirm the intended config keeps your management access.

  6. Validate with ANTA: write an ANTA inventory for the six switches, merge the six AVD catalogs, run anta nrfu and get a clean result (explain any test that cannot pass on cEOS). Prove the data plane from the hosts too.

  7. Explain what AVD's own validation role adds on top of running ANTA by hand.

  8. Day 2 through the data model: add VLAN 40 (name WEB, 10.40.40.1/24) to TENANT-A and allow it to host1 and host2, rebuild, review the diff, deploy, and validate. Do not touch the switches by hand. Then put VLAN 40 on host1/host2 and prove reachability.

  9. Revert the Day-2 change the same way (git revert, rebuild, redeploy). Capstone C2 reuses VLAN 40 for a different tenant.

Solution · task 2: build with pyavd 
$ cd labs/avd
$ python3 build.py
built spine1
built spine2
built leaf1
built leaf2
built leaf3
built leaf4
$ ls intended/*
intended/configs:            leaf1.cfg leaf2.cfg leaf3.cfg leaf4.cfg spine1.cfg spine2.cfg
intended/structured_configs: leaf1.yml leaf2.yml ... spine2.yml
intended/test_catalogs:      leaf1-catalog.yml ... spine2-catalog.yml
$ git status --short intended/     # empty means your build matches the committed reference

build.py does what the Ansible roles do: it flattens the inventory into per-host variables (outermost group first, like Ansible), validates each host's inputs against the AVD schema (pyavd.validate_inputs), computes fabric-wide facts (get_avd_facts: who peers with whom, which IPs come from which pool), then produces structured config, CLI and the ANTA catalog per device.

Solution · task 3: structured config vs CLI 
$ grep -n -A6 '^vxlan_interface' intended/structured_configs/leaf3.yml
vxlan_interface:
  vxlan1:
    description: leaf3_VTEP
    vxlan:
      source_interface: Loopback1
      udp_port: 4789
      virtual_router_encapsulation_mac_address: mlag-system-id

eos_designs knows about fabrics (pools, roles, peers) and outputs a device-level data structure that mirrors EOS features one to one. eos_cli_config_gen knows nothing about fabrics; it only renders that structure to CLI with templates. When a line is wrong, check the structured config first: if the value is wrong there, the cause is in your inputs or in the design logic; if it is right there but wrong in the CLI, it is a rendering question. The structured config is also what you override with custom_structured_configuration_* or structured_config when the design does not offer a knob, as the structured_config block in L5DC_FABRIC.yml does for eAPI and gNMI. (A top-level management_api_http key in group_vars is not an eos_designs input and is silently ignored; the structured_config route is the reliable one.)

Solution · task 4: build with Ansible (playbook text) 
$ pip install ansible-core "pyavd[ansible]==6.4.*"
$ ansible-galaxy collection install arista.avd    # pin the version that matches pyavd
$ cat labs/avd/playbooks/build.yml
---
- name: Build L5DC fabric configs
  hosts: L5DC_FABRIC
  gather_facts: false
  connection: local
  vars:
    root_dir: "{{ playbook_dir }}/.."
  tasks:
    - name: Generate structured configuration (eos_designs)
      ansible.builtin.import_role:
        name: arista.avd.eos_designs

    - name: Render EOS CLI and device documentation (eos_cli_config_gen)
      ansible.builtin.import_role:
        name: arista.avd.eos_cli_config_gen
$ cd labs/avd
$ ansible-playbook -i inventory.yml playbooks/build.yml
PLAY RECAP *********************************************************************
leaf1  : ok=..  changed=..  unreachable=0  failed=0
...
$ git status --short intended/configs   # same configs as build.py

root_dir points the output at labs/avd/intended/ (the default is the playbook's own folder). Ansible also writes per-device and fabric documentation (Markdown) under labs/avd/documentation/, which pyavd can do too but build.py skips.

Solution · task 5: deploy (three ways)

First the safety check: a full replace removes every line that is not in the intended config. If the intended config had no management api http-commands, you would lose eAPI (and with it Ansible, ANTA and the Python tools) the moment it applies.

$ grep -c -E '^management api (http-commands|gnmi)' intended/configs/*.cfg
intended/configs/leaf1.cfg:2
...
intended/configs/spine2.cfg:2

Way 1, Ansible (labs/avd/playbooks/deploy.yml): the eos_config_deploy_eapi role does a config replace through an eAPI configuration session and saves the config. Run it with --check --diff first to see what would change.

---
- name: Deploy L5DC fabric configs
  hosts: L5DC_FABRIC
  gather_facts: false
  vars:
    root_dir: "{{ playbook_dir }}/.."
  tasks:
    - name: Replace running-config with the AVD intended config
      ansible.builtin.import_role:
        name: arista.avd.eos_config_deploy_eapi
$ ansible-playbook -i inventory.yml playbooks/deploy.yml --check --diff
$ ansible-playbook -i inventory.yml playbooks/deploy.yml

Way 2, Python and eAPI, the same mechanism without Ansible: open a session, start from a clean config, load the intended lines, show the diff, commit, save. Save it as labs/avd/deploy_eapi.py (your own file; it is not in the repo).

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""deploy_eapi.py - config replace each switch with its AVD intended config."""
import sys, pathlib, requests, urllib3
urllib3.disable_warnings()
HOSTS = {"spine1": "172.100.100.11", "spine2": "172.100.100.12",
         "leaf1": "172.100.100.21", "leaf2": "172.100.100.22",
         "leaf3": "172.100.100.23", "leaf4": "172.100.100.24"}
DRY = "--dry-run" in sys.argv

def eapi(ip, cmds):
    r = requests.post(f"https://{ip}/command-api", auth=("admin", "admin"), verify=False, timeout=60,
                      json={"jsonrpc": "2.0", "method": "runCmds", "id": 1,
                            "params": {"version": 1, "format": "text", "cmds": cmds}})
    body = r.json()
    if "error" in body:
        raise RuntimeError(body["error"]["message"])
    return body["result"]

for name, ip in HOSTS.items():
    cfg = pathlib.Path(f"intended/configs/{name}.cfg").read_text().splitlines()
    lines = [l for l in cfg if l.strip() and not l.startswith("!") and l.strip() != "end"]
    cmds = ["enable", "configure session avd-deploy", "rollback clean-config", *lines,
            "show session-config diffs"]
    cmds += ["abort"] if DRY else ["commit", "copy running-config startup-config"]
    out = eapi(ip, cmds)
    diff = out[len(cmds) - (2 if DRY else 3)]["output"]
    print(f"===== {name}\n{diff or '(no change)'}")
$ cd labs/avd && python3 deploy_eapi.py --dry-run    # review every diff
$ python3 deploy_eapi.py

Way 3, file and configure replace, handy for one device:

$ sudo docker cp intended/configs/leaf1.cfg clab-l5dc-leaf1:/mnt/flash/intended.cfg
$ ssh admin@172.100.100.21
leaf1# configure replace flash:intended.cfg
leaf1# write memory

Deploy order: spines first, then one leaf of each MLAG pair, then the other. In a greenfield build the order does not matter; on a live fabric it does (Capstone C4).

Solution · task 6: ANTA inventory, catalog and run

The inventory is in the repo as labs/avd/anta-inventory.yml:

---
anta_inventory:
  hosts:
    - name: spine1
      host: 172.100.100.11
      tags: ["spine"]
    - name: spine2
      host: 172.100.100.12
      tags: ["spine"]
    - name: leaf1
      host: 172.100.100.21
      tags: ["leaf", "POD_A"]
    - name: leaf2
      host: 172.100.100.22
      tags: ["leaf", "POD_A"]
    - name: leaf3
      host: 172.100.100.23
      tags: ["leaf", "POD_B", "border"]
    - name: leaf4
      host: 172.100.100.24
      tags: ["leaf", "POD_B", "border"]

ANTA gives every device its own name as an implicit tag, and every test in the AVD catalogs carries filters: tags: [leaf3], so one merged catalog runs the right tests on the right switch. anta nrfu takes a single -c file, so merge first:

$ cd labs/avd
$ python3 -c "import glob; from anta.catalog import AntaCatalog as C; \
print(C.merge_catalogs([C.parse(f) for f in sorted(glob.glob('intended/test_catalogs/*-catalog.yml'))]).dump().yaml())" \
  > fabric-catalog.yml
$ anta nrfu -u admin -p admin -i anta-inventory.yml -c fabric-catalog.yml --dry-run table
  - ANTA Inventory contains 6 devices (AsyncEOSDevice)
  - Tests catalog contains 144 tests
$ anta nrfu -u admin -p admin -i anta-inventory.yml -c fabric-catalog.yml table --group-by device
                              Summary per device
┏━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━┳━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓
